L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     issorted(itr, lt=isless, by=identity, rev::Bool=false, order::Base.Order.Ordering=Base.Order.Forward)
T 105 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Test whether a collection is in sorted order. The keywords modify what
T 70 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L order is considered sorted, as described in the [`sort!`](@ref) documentation.
T 78 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L As for `sort!`, `lt` must define a strict weak order, otherwise the result may
T 78 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L be unexpected: `issorted` only checks that no element is less than the one
T 74 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L before it. For example, `issorted([1, 1], lt=(<=))` returns `false`.
T 68 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L # Examples
T 10 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```jldoctest
T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L julia> issorted([1, 2, 3])
T 26 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L true
T 4 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> issorted([(1, "b"), (2, "a")], by = x -> x[1])
T 53 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L true
T 4 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> issorted([(1, "b"), (2, "a")], by = x -> x[2])
T 53 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L false
T 5 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> issorted([(1, "b"), (2, "a")], by = x -> x[2], rev=true)
T 63 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L true
T 4 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> issorted([1, 2, -2, 3], by=abs)
T 38 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L true
T 4 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.end.julia

L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     partialsort!(v, k; by=identity, lt=isless, rev=false, order::Base.Order.Ordering=Base.Order.Forward)
T 104 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Mutate the vector `v` so that the value at index `k` (or
T 56 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L range of adjacent values if `k` is a range) occurs
T 50 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L at the position where it would appear if the array were fully sorted. If `k` is a single
T 88 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L index, that value is returned; if `k` is a range, an array of values at those indices is
T 88 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L returned. Note that `partialsort!` may not fully sort the input array.
T 70 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L For the keyword arguments, see the documentation of [`sort!`](@ref).
T 68 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L 
L # Examples
T 10 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```jldoctest
T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L julia> a = [1, 2, 4, 3, 4]
T 26 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 5-element Vector{Int64}:
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  1
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  2
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  4
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  3
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  4
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> partialsort!(a, 4)
T 25 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 4
T 1 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> a
T 8 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 5-element Vector{Int64}:
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  1
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  2
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  3
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  4
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  4
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> a = [1, 2, 4, 3, 4]
T 26 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 5-element Vector{Int64}:
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  1
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  2

L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     searchsorted(v, x; by=identity, lt=isless, rev=false)
T 57 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Return the range of indices in `v` where values are equivalent to `x`, or an
T 76 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L empty range located at the insertion point if `v` does not contain values
T 73 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L equivalent to `x`. The vector `v` must be sorted according to the order defined
T 79 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L by the keywords. Refer to [`sort!`](@ref) for the meaning of the keywords and
T 77 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L the definition of equivalence. Note that the `by` function is applied to the
T 76 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L searched value `x` as well as the values in `v`.
T 48 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L The range is generally found using binary search, but there are optimized
T 73 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L implementations for some inputs.
T 32 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L See also [`searchsortedfirst`](@ref), [`sort!`](@ref), [`insorted`](@ref), [`findall`](@ref).
T 93 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L # Examples

T 62 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Return the index of the first value in `v` that is not ordered before `x`.
T 74 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L If all values in `v` are ordered before `x`, return `lastindex(v) + 1`.
T 71 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L

T 77 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L `insert!`ing `x` at the returned index will maintain the sorted order.
T 70 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L Refer to [`sort!`](@ref) for the meaning and use of the keywords.
T 65 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L Note that the `by` function is applied to the searched value `x` as well as the
T 79 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L values in `v`.
T 14 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L The index is generally found using binary search, but there are optimized
T 73 source.julia string.docstring.julia

T 72 source.julia string.docstring.julia

L Return `(s, t)` where `t` is an `AbstractVector` of type `T` with length at least `len`
T 87 source.julia string.docstring.julia

T 83 source.julia string.docstring.julia

T 92 source.julia string.docstring.julia

T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     _sort!(v::AbstractVector, a::Base.Sort.Algorithm, o::Base.Order.Ordering, kw; t, offset)
T 92 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L An internal function that sorts `v` using the algorithm `a` under the ordering `o`,
T 83 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L subject to specifications provided in `kw` (such as `lo` and `hi` in which case it only
T 87 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L sorts `view(v, lo:hi)`)
T 23 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L

T 83 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L no scratch space is present.
T 28 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L !!! note
T 8 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     `_sort!` modifies but does not return `v`.
T 46 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L A returned scratch space will be a `Vector{T}` where `T` is usually the eltype of `v`. There
T 92 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L are some exceptions, for example if `eltype(v) == Union{Missing, T}` then the scratch space
T 91 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L may be a `Vector{T}` due to `MissingOptimization` changing the eltype of `v` to `T`.
T 84 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L `t` is an appropriate scratch space for the algorithm at hand, to be accessed as
T 80 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L `t[i + offset]`. `t` is used for an algorithm to pass a scratch space back to itself in
T 87 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L internal or recursive calls.

L     IEEEFloatOptimization(next) isa Base.Sort.Algorithm
T 55 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Move NaN values to the end, partition by sign, and reinterpret the rest as unsigned integers.
T 93 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L IEEE floating point numbers (`Float64`, `Float32`, and `Float16`) compare the same as
T 85 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L unsigned integers with the bits with a few exceptions.
T 54 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L

T 88 source.julia string.docstring.julia

L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     InsertionSort
T 17 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Use the insertion sort algorithm.
T 33 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Insertion sort traverses the collection one element at a time, inserting
T 72 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L each element into its correct, sorted position in the output vector.
T 68 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Characteristics:
T 16 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L * *stable*: preserves the ordering of elements that compare equal
T 65 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L (e.g. "a" and "A" in a sort of letters that ignores case).
T 58 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L * *in-place* in memory.
T 23 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L * *quadratic performance* in the number of elements to be sorted:
T 65 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L it is well-suited to small collections but should not be used for large ones.
T 77 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.end.julia
L const InsertionSort = InsertionSortAlg()
T 5 source.julia keyword.storage.modifier.julia
T 20 source.julia
T 21 source.julia keyword.operator.update.julia
T 22 source.julia

T 40 source.julia meta.bracket.julia

L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     RadixSort() isa Base.Sort.Algorithm
T 39 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Use the radix sort algorithm.
T 29 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L `RadixSort` is a stable least significant bit first radix sort algorithm that runs in
T 85 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L `O(length * log(range))` time and linear space.
T 47 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L It first sorts the entire vector by the last `chunk_size` bits, then by the second
T 82 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L to last `chunk_size` bits, and so on. Stability means that it will not reorder two elements
T 91 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L that compare equal. This is essential so that the order introduced by earlier,
T 78 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L less significant passes is preserved by later passes.
T 53 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Each pass divides the input into `2^chunk_size == mask+1` buckets. To do this, it
T 81 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  * counts the number of entries that fall into each bucket
T 58 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  * uses those counts to compute the indices to move elements of those buckets into
T 82 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  * moves elements into the computed indices in the swap array
T 61 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  * switches the swap and working array
T 38 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L `chunk_size` is larger for larger inputs and determined by an empirical heuristic.
T 82 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.end.julia

L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     ScratchQuickSort(next::Base.Sort.Algorithm=Base.Sort.SMALL_ALGORITHM) isa Base.Sort.Algorithm
T 97 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     ScratchQuickSort(lo::Union{Integer, Missing}, hi::Union{Integer, Missing}=lo, next::Base.Sort.Algorithm=Base.Sort.SMALL_ALGORITHM) isa Base.Sort.Algorithm
T 158 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Use the `ScratchQuickSort` algorithm with the `next` algorithm as a base case.
T 78 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L `ScratchQuickSort` is like `QuickSort`, but utilizes scratch space to operate faster and allow
T 94 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L for the possibility of maintaining stability.
T 45 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L If `lo` and `hi` are provided, finds and sorts the elements in the range `lo:hi`, reordering
T 92 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L but not necessarily sorting other elements in the process. If `lo` or `hi` is `missing`, it
T 91 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L is treated as the first or last index of the input, respectively.
T 65 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L `lo` and `hi` may be specified together as an `AbstractUnitRange`.
T 66 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Characteristics:
T 16 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   * *stable*: preserves the ordering of elements that compare equal
T 67 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     (e.g. "a" and "A" in a sort of letters that ignores case).
T 62 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   * *not in-place* in memory.
T 29 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   * *divide-and-conquer*: sort strategy similar to [`QuickSort`](@ref).
T 71 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   * *linear runtime* if `length(lo:hi)` is constant
T 51 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   * *quadratic worst case runtime* in pathological cases
T 56 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   (vanishingly rare for non-malicious input)
T 44 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.end.julia

L 
L 
L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     BracketedSort(target[, next::Algorithm]) isa Base.Sort.Algorithm
T 68 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Perform a partialsort for the elements that fall into the indices specified by the `target`
T 91 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L using BracketedSort with the `next` algorithm for subproblems.
T 62 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L BracketedSort takes a random* sample of the input, estimates the quantiles of the input
T 87 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L using the quantiles of the sample to find signposts that almost certainly bracket the target
T 92 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L values, filters the value in the input that fall between the signpost values to the front of
T 92 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L the input, and then, if that "almost certainly" turned out to be true, finds the target
T 87 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L within the small chunk that are, by value, between the signposts and now by position, at the
T 92 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L front of the vector. On small inputs or when target is close to the size of the input,
T 86 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L BracketedSort falls back to the `next` algorithm directly. Otherwise, BracketedSort uses the
T 92 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L `next` algorithm only to compute quantiles of the sample and to find the target within the
T 90 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L small chunk.
T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L ## Performance
T 14 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L If the `next` algorithm has `O(n * log(n))` runtime and the input is not pathological then
T 90 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L the runtime of this algorithm is `O(n + k * log(k))` where `n` is the length of the input
T 89 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L and `k` is `length(target)`. On pathological inputs the asymptotic runtime is the same as
T 89 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L the runtime of the `next` algorithm.
T 36 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L BracketedSort itself does not allocate. If `next` is in-place then BracketedSort is also
T 88 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L in-place. If `next` is not in place, and it's space usage increases monotonically with input
T 92 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L length then BracketedSort's maximum space usage will never be more than the space usage
T 87 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L of `next` on the input BracketedSort receives. For large nonpathological inputs and targets
T 91 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L substantially smaller than the size of the input, BracketedSort's maximum memory usage will
T 91 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L be much less than `next`'s. If the maximum additional space usage of `next` scales linearly
T 91 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L then for small k the average* maximum additional space usage of BracketedSort will be
T 85 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L `O(n^(2.3/3))`.
T 15 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L By default, BracketedSort uses the `O(n)` space and `O(n + k log k)` runtime
T 76 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L `ScratchQuickSort` algorithm recursively.
T 41 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L *Sorting is unable to depend on Random.jl because Random.jl depends on sorting.
T 79 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  Consequently, we use `hash` as a source of randomness. The average runtime guarantees
T 86 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  assume that `hash(x::Int)` produces a random result. However, as this randomization is
T 87 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  deterministic, if you try hard enough you can find inputs that consistently reach the
T 86 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  worst case bounds. Actually constructing such inputs is an exercise left to the reader.
T 88 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  Have fun :).
T 13 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Characteristics:
T 16 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   * *unstable*: does not preserve the ordering of elements that compare equal
T 77 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     (e.g. "a" and "A" in a sort of letters that ignores case).
T 62 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   * *in-place* in memory if the `next` algorithm is in-place.
T 61 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   * *estimate-and-filter*: strategy
T 35 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   * *linear runtime* if `length(target)` is constant and `next` is reasonable
T 77 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   * *n + k log k* worst case runtime if `next` has that runtime.
T 64 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   * *pathological inputs* can significantly increase constant factors.
T 70 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.end.julia

T 4 source.julia

L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     InitialOptimizations(next) isa Base.Sort.Algorithm
T 54 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Attempt to apply a suite of low-cost optimizations to the input vector before sorting. These
T 92 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L optimizations may be automatically applied by the `sort!` family of functions when
T 82 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L `alg=InsertionSort`, `alg=MergeSort`, or `alg=QuickSort` is passed as an argument.
T 82 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L `InitialOptimizations` is an implementation detail and subject to change or removal in
T 86 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L future versions of Julia.
T 25 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L If `next` is stable, then `InitialOptimizations(next)` is also stable.
T 70 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L The specific optimizations attempted by `InitialOptimizations` are
T 66 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L [`SubArrayOptimization`](@ref), [`MissingOptimization`](@ref), [`BoolOptimization`](@ref),
T 90 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L dispatch to [`InsertionSort`](@ref) for inputs with `length <= 10`, and
T 71 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L [`IEEEFloatOptimization`](@ref).
T 32 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L """

L 
L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     struct DefaultStable <: Algorithm end
T 41 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L `DefaultStable` is an algorithm which indicates that a fast, general purpose sorting
T 84 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L algorithm should be used, but does not specify exactly which algorithm.
T 71 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Currently, when sorting short NTuples, this is an unrolled mergesort, and otherwise it is
T 89 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L composed of two parts: the [`InitialOptimizations`](@ref) and a hybrid of Radix, Insertion,
T 91 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L Counting, Quick sorts.
T 22 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L We begin with MissingOptimization because it has no runtime cost when it is not
T 79 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L triggered and can enable other optimizations to be applied later. For example,
T 78 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L BoolOptimization cannot apply to an `AbstractVector{Union{Missing, Bool}}`, but after
T 85 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L [`MissingOptimization`](@ref) is applied, that input will be converted into am
T 78 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L `AbstractVector{Bool}`.
T 23 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L We next apply [`BoolOptimization`](@ref) because it also has no runtime cost when it is not
T 91 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L triggered and when it is triggered, it is an incredibly efficient algorithm (sorting `Bool`s
T 92 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L is quite easy).
T 15 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Next, we dispatch to [`InsertionSort`](@ref) for inputs with `length <= 10`. This dispatch
T 90 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L occurs before the [`IEEEFloatOptimization`](@ref) pass because the
T 66 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L [`IEEEFloatOptimization`](@ref)s are not beneficial for very small inputs.
T 74 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L To conclude the [`InitialOptimizations`](@ref), we apply [`IEEEFloatOptimization`](@ref).
T 89 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L After these optimizations, we branch on whether radix sort and related algorithms can be
T 88 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L applied to the input vector and ordering. We conduct this branch by testing if
T 78 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L `UIntMappable(v, order) !== nothing`. That is, we see if we know of a reversible mapping
T 88 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L from `eltype(v)` to `UInt` that preserves the ordering `order`. We perform this check after
T 91 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L the initial optimizations because they can change the input vector's type and ordering to
T 89 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L make them `UIntMappable`.
T 25 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L If the input is not [`UIntMappable`](@ref), then we perform a presorted check and dispatch
T 90 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L to [`ScratchQuickSort`](@ref).
T 30 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Otherwise, we dispatch to [`InsertionSort`](@ref) for inputs with `length <= 40` and then
T 89 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L perform a presorted check ([`CheckSorted`](@ref)).
T 50 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L We check for short inputs before performing the presorted check to avoid the overhead of the
T 92 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L check for small inputs. Because the alternate dispatch is to [`InsertionSort`](@ref) which
T 90 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L has efficient `O(n)` runtime on presorted inputs, the check is not necessary for small
T 86 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L inputs.
T 7 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L We check if the input is reverse-sorted for long vectors (more than 500 elements) because
T 89 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L the check is essentially free unless the input is almost entirely reverse sorted.
T 81 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Note that once the input is determined to be [`UIntMappable`](@ref), we know the order forms
T 92 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L a [total order](wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_order) over the inputs and so it is impossible to
T 90 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L perform an unstable sort because no two elements can compare equal unless they _are_ equal,
T 91 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L in which case switching them is undetectable. We utilize this fact to perform a more
T 84 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L aggressive reverse sorted check that will reverse the vector `[3, 2, 2, 1]`.
T 76 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L After these potential fast-paths are tried and failed, we [`ComputeExtrema`](@ref) of the
T 89 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L input. This computation has a fairly fast `O(n)` runtime, but we still try to delay it until
T 92 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L it is necessary.
T 16 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Next, we [`ConsiderCountingSort`](@ref). If the range the input is small compared to its
T 88 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L length, we apply [`CountingSort`](@ref).
T 40 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Next, we [`ConsiderRadixSort`](@ref). This is similar to the dispatch to counting sort,
T 87 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L but we consider the number of _bits_ in the range, rather than the range itself.
T 80 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L Consequently, we apply [`RadixSort`](@ref) for any reasonably long inputs that reach this
T 89 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L stage.
T 6 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Finally, if the input has length less than 80, we dispatch to [`InsertionSort`](@ref) and
T 89 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L otherwise we dispatch to [`ScratchQuickSort`](@ref).
T 52 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.end.julia
L struct DefaultStable <: Algorithm end
T 6 source.julia keyword.other.julia
T 20 source.julia

L defalg(v) = DEFAULT_STABLE
T 6 source.julia entity.name.function.julia
T 7 source.julia meta.bracket.julia

T 9 source.julia meta.bracket.julia

L 
L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     sort!(v; alg::Base.Sort.Algorithm=Base.Sort.defalg(v), lt=isless, by=identity, rev::Bool=false, order::Base.Order.Ordering=Base.Order.Forward)
T 146 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Mutate the vector `v` so that it is sorted.
T 43 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L A stable algorithm is used by default: the ordering of elements that
T 68 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L compare equal is preserved. A specific algorithm can be selected via the
T 72 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L `alg` keyword (see [Sorting Algorithms](@ref) for available algorithms).
T 72 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Elements are first transformed with the function `by` and then compared
T 71 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L according to either the function `lt` or the ordering `order`. Finally, the
T 75 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L resulting order is reversed if `rev=true` (this preserves forward stability:
T 76 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L elements that compare equal are not reversed). The current implementation applies
T 81 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L the `by` transformation before each comparison rather than once per element.
T 76 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Passing an `lt` other than `isless` along with an `order` other than
T 68 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L [`Base.Order.Forward`](@ref) or [`Base.Order.Reverse`](@ref) is not permitted,
T 78 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L otherwise all options are independent and can be used together in all possible
T 78 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L combinations. Note that `order` can also include a "by" transformation, in
T 74 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L which case it is applied after that defined with the `by` keyword. For more
T 75 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L information on `order` values see the documentation on [Alternate
T 65 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L Orderings](@ref).
T 17 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Relations between two elements are defined as follows (with "less" and
T 70 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L "greater" exchanged when `rev=true`):
T 37 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L * `x` is less than `y` if `lt(by(x), by(y))` (or `Base.Order.lt(order, by(x), by(y))`) yields true.
T 99 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L * `x` is greater than `y` if `y` is less than `x`.
T 50 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L * `x` and `y` are equivalent if neither is less than the other ("incomparable"
T 78 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   is sometimes used as a synonym for "equivalent").
T 51 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L The result of `sort!` is sorted in the sense that every element is greater than
T 79 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L or equivalent to the previous one.
T 34 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L The `lt` function must define a strict weak order, that is, it must be
T 70 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L * irreflexive: `lt(x, x)` always yields `false`,
T 48 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L * asymmetric: if `lt(x, y)` yields `true` then `lt(y, x)` yields `false`,
T 73 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L * transitive: `lt(x, y) && lt(y, z)` implies `lt(x, z)`,
T 56 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L * transitive in equivalence: `!lt(x, y) && !lt(y, x)` and `!lt(y, z) && !lt(z,
T 78 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   y)` together imply `!lt(x, z) && !lt(z, x)`. In words: if `x` and `y` are
T 75 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   equivalent and `y` and `z` are equivalent then `x` and `z` must be
T 68 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   equivalent.
T 13 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L For example `<` is a valid `lt` function for `Int` values but `≤` is not: it
T 78 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L violates irreflexivity. For `Float64` values even `<` is invalid as it violates
T 79 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L the fourth condition: `1.0` and `NaN` are equivalent and so are `NaN` and `2.0`
T 79 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L but `1.0` and `2.0` are not equivalent.
T 39 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L See also [`sort`](@ref), [`sortperm`](@ref), [`sortslices`](@ref),
T 66 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L [`partialsort!`](@ref), [`partialsortperm`](@ref), [`issorted`](@ref),
T 70 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L [`searchsorted`](@ref), [`insorted`](@ref), [`Base.Order.ord`](@ref).
T 69 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L # Examples
T 10 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```jldoctest
T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L julia> v = [3, 1, 2]; sort!(v); v
T 33 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 3-element Vector{Int64}:
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  1
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  2
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  3
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> v = [3, 1, 2]; sort!(v, rev = true); v
T 45 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 3-element Vector{Int64}:
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  3
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  2
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  1
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> v = [(1, "c"), (3, "a"), (2, "b")]; sort!(v, by = x -> x[1]); v
T 70 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 3-element Vector{Tuple{Int64, String}}:
T 39 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  (1, "c")
T 9 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  (2, "b")
T 9 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  (3, "a")
T 9 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> v = [(1, "c"), (3, "a"), (2, "b")]; sort!(v, by = x -> x[2]); v
T 70 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 3-element Vector{Tuple{Int64, String}}:
T 39 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  (3, "a")
T 9 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  (2, "b")
T 9 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  (1, "c")
T 9 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> sort(0:3, by=x->x-2, order=Base.Order.By(abs))
T 53 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 4-element Vector{Int64}:
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  2
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  1
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  3
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  0
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> sort(0:3, by=x->x-2, order=Base.Order.By(abs)) == sort(0:3, by=x->abs(x-2))
T 82 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L true
T 4 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> sort([2, NaN, 1, NaN, 3]) # correct sort with default lt=isless
T 70 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 5-element Vector{Float64}:
T 26 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L    1.0
T 6 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L    2.0
T 6 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L    3.0
T 6 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  NaN
T 4 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  NaN
T 4 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> sort([2, NaN, 1, NaN, 3], lt=<) # wrong sort due to invalid lt. This behavior is undefined.
T 98 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 5-element Vector{Float64}:
T 26 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L    2.0
T 6 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  NaN
T 4 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L    1.0
T 6 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  NaN
T 4 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L    3.0
T 6 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.end.julia
L function sort!(v::AbstractVector{T};
T 8 source.julia keyword.other.julia
T 9 source.julia

T 20 source.julia keyword.operator.relation.types.julia
T 29 source.julia support.type.julia
T 30 source.julia keyword.operator.update.julia
T 36 source.julia support.function.julia

T 18 source.julia

T 145 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     sort(v::NTuple; kws...)::NTuple
T 35 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Variant of [`sort!`](@ref) that returns a sorted copy of `v` leaving `v` itself unmodified.
T 91 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L When calling `sort` on the [`keys`](@ref) or [`values`](@ref) of a dictionary, `v` is
T 85 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L collected and then sorted.
T 26 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L !!! compat "Julia 1.12"
T 23 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     Sorting `NTuple`s requires Julia 1.12 or later.
T 51 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L !!! compat "Julia 1.13"
T 23 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     Sorting keys sets and values iterators requires Julia 1.13 or later.
T 72 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L # Examples
T 10 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```jldoctest
T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L julia> v = [3, 1, 2];
T 21 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> sort(v)
T 14 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 3-element Vector{Int64}:
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  1
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  2
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  3
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> v
T 8 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 3-element Vector{Int64}:
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  3
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  1
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  2
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> sort(values(Dict('a'=>2, 'b'=>1)))
T 41 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 2-element Vector{Int64}:
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia

T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  2

L 
L ## partialsortperm: the permutation to sort the first k elements of an array ##
T 1 source.julia comment.line.number-sign.julia punctuation.definition.comment.julia
T 79 source.julia comment.line.number-sign.julia
L 
L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     partialsortperm(v, k; by=identity, lt=isless, rev=false)
T 60 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Return a partial permutation `I` of the vector `v`, so that `v[I]` returns values of a fully
T 92 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L sorted version of `v` at index `k`. If `k` is a range, a vector of indices is returned; if
T 90 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L `k` is an integer, a single index is returned. The order is specified using the same
T 84 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L keywords as `sort!`. The permutation is stable: the indices of equal elements
T 77 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L will appear in ascending order.
T 31 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L This function is equivalent to, but more efficient than, calling `sortperm(...)[k]`.
T 84 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L # Examples
T 10 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```jldoctest
T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L julia> v = [3, 1, 2, 1];
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> v[partialsortperm(v, 1)]
T 31 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 1
T 1 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> p = partialsortperm(v, 1:3)
T 34 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 3-element view(::Vector{Int64}, 1:3) with eltype Int64:
T 55 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  2
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  4
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  3
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> v[p]
T 11 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 3-element Vector{Int64}:
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  1
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  1
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  2
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia

L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     partialsortperm!(ix, v, k; by=identity, lt=isless, rev=false)
T 65 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Like [`partialsortperm`](@ref), but accepts a preallocated index vector `ix` the same size as
T 93 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L `v`, which is used to store (a permutation of) the indices of `v`.
T 66 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L `ix` is initialized to contain the indices of `v`.
T 50 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L (Typically, the indices of `v` will be `1:length(v)`, although if `v` has an alternative array type
T 99 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L with non-one-based indices, such as an `OffsetArray`, `ix` must share those same indices)
T 89 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Upon return, `ix` is guaranteed to have the indices `k` in their sorted positions, such that
T 92 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L ```julia
T 8 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L partialsortperm!(ix, v, k);
T 27 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L v[ix[k]] == partialsort(v, k)
T 29 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L The return value is the `k`th element of `ix` if `k` is an integer, or view into `ix` if `k` is
T 95 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L a range.
T 8 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L $(Base._DOCS_ALIASING_WARNING)
T 1 source.julia string.docstring.julia variable.interpolation.julia
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia variable.interpolation.julia meta.bracket.julia
T 6 source.julia string.docstring.julia variable.interpolation.julia
T 7 source.julia string.docstring.julia variable.interpolation.julia keyword.operator.dots.julia
T 29 source.julia string.docstring.julia variable.interpolation.julia
T 30 source.julia string.docstring.julia variable.interpolation.julia meta.bracket.julia
L 
L # Examples
T 10 source.julia string.docstring.julia

T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L julia> v = [3, 1, 2, 1];
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia

L 
L ## sortperm: the permutation to sort an array ##
T 1 source.julia comment.line.number-sign.julia punctuation.definition.comment.julia
T 48 source.julia comment.line.number-sign.julia
L 
L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     sortperm(A; alg::Base.Sort.Algorithm=Base.Sort.DEFAULT_UNSTABLE, lt=isless, by=identity, rev::Bool=false, order::Base.Order.Ordering=Base.Order.Forward, [dims::Integer])
T 173 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Return a permutation vector or array `I` that puts `A[I]` in sorted order along the given dimension.
T 100 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L If `A` has more than one dimension, then the `dims` keyword argument must be specified. The order is specified
T 110 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L using the same keywords as [`sort!`](@ref). The permutation is guaranteed to be stable even
T 91 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L if the sorting algorithm is unstable: the indices of equal elements will appear in
T 82 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ascending order.
T 16 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L See also [`sortperm!`](@ref), [`partialsortperm`](@ref), [`invperm`](@ref), [`indexin`](@ref).
T 94 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L To sort slices of an array, refer to [`sortslices`](@ref).
T 58 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L !!! compat "Julia 1.9"
T 22 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     The method accepting `dims` requires at least Julia 1.9.
T 60 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L # Examples
T 10 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```jldoctest
T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L julia> v = [13, 11, 12];
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> p = sortperm(v)
T 22 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 3-element Vector{Int64}:
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  2
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  3
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  1
T 2 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> v[p]
T 11 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 3-element Vector{Int64}:
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  11
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  12
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  13
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> A = [8 7; 5 6]
T 21 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 2×2 Matrix{Int64}:
T 19 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  8  7
T 5 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  5  6
T 5 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> sortperm(A, dims = 1)
T 28 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 2×2 Matrix{Int64}:
T 19 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  2  4
T 5 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  1  3
T 5 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> sortperm(A, dims = 2)
T 28 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 2×2 Matrix{Int64}:

L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     PartialQuickSort{T <: Union{Integer,OrdinalRange}}
T 54 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Indicate that a sorting function should use the partial quick sort algorithm.
T 77 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L `PartialQuickSort(k)` is like `QuickSort`, but is only required to find and
T 75 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L sort the elements that would end up in `v[k]` were `v` fully sorted.
T 68 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Characteristics:
T 16 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   * *not stable*: does not preserve the ordering of elements that
T 65 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     compare equal (e.g. "a" and "A" in a sort of letters that
T 61 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     ignores case).
T 18 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   * *in-place* in memory.
T 25 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   * *divide-and-conquer*: sort strategy similar to [`QuickSort`](@ref).
T 71 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Note that `PartialQuickSort(k)` does not necessarily sort the whole array. For example,
T 87 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L ```jldoctest
T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L julia> x = rand(100);
T 21 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> k = 50:100;
T 18 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> s1 = sort(x; alg=QuickSort);
T 35 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> s2 = sort(x; alg=PartialQuickSort(k));
T 45 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> map(issorted, (s1, s2))
T 30 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L (true, false)
T 13 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> map(x->issorted(x[k]), (s1, s2))
T 39 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L (true, true)
T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> s1[k] == s2[k]
T 21 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L true
T 4 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.end.julia

T 58 source.julia string.docstring.julia

T 49 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L subcollections and repeatedly merges them, sorting each
T 55 source.julia string.docstring.julia

T 44 source.julia string.docstring.julia

T 46 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Characteristics:
T 16 source.julia string.docstring.julia

T 61 source.julia string.docstring.julia

T 61 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     case).
T 10 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   * *not in-place* in memory.
T 29 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L   * *divide-and-conquer* sort strategy.
T 39 source.julia string.docstring.julia

T 71 source.julia string.docstring.julia

T 32 source.julia string.docstring.julia
